      "content": "# T1: SDPI Parallel Channel Composition\n\n## Setting\n\nConsider a multi-judge evaluation protocol where $K$ judges independently score the same input.\n\n| Symbol | Definition |\n|--------|-----------|\n| $\\mathcal{X}$ | Input space (e.g., response pairs) |\n| $\\mathcal{Y}$ | Score/verdict space |\n| $X \\in \\mathcal{X}$ | Input to be evaluated |\n| $W_k: \\mathcal{X} \\to \\mathcal{Y}$ | Judge $k$'s stochastic channel, $k = 1, \\ldots, K$ |\n| $Y_k = W_k(X)$ | Judge $k$'s output |\n| $\\eta_k \\in [0,1]$ | SDPI contraction coefficient of $W_k$: $\\eta_k := \\sup_{P \\neq Q} \\frac{D_{KL}(W_k P \\| W_k Q)}{D_{KL}(P \\| Q)}$ |\n| $\\eta_{\\max}$ | $\\max_k \\eta_k$ |\n| $I(Y_i; Y_j)$ | Pairwise mutual information between judge outputs (under reference input distribution) |\n| $I_{\\max}$ | $\\max_{i \\neq j} I(Y_i; Y_j)$ |\n| $f(Y_1, \\ldots, Y_K)$ | Panel aggregation rule (e.g., majority vote) |\n\n**Attacker's action**: Replace the input distribution $P_X$ with a manipulated distribution $Q_X$, incurring cost $\\Delta := D_{KL}(Q_X \\| P_X)$, with the goal of flipping $f(Y^K)$.\n\n**Channel model**: The joint channel $W^{(K)}: X \\to (Y_1, \\ldots, Y_K)$ broadcasts a single input to $K$ judges. Judges may be correlated—both through the shared input $X$ (which induces marginal dependence) and through shared biases (training data overlap, architectural similarities), which induce conditional dependence $I(Y_i; Y_j \\mid X) > 0$.\n\n---\n\n## Key Distinction: Serial vs Parallel\n\n### Serial Chain (Markov cascade)\n\nIf judges formed a processing pipeline $X \\to Y_1 \\to Y_2 \\to \\cdots \\to Y_K$ (each judge only sees the previous judge's output):\n\n$$D_{KL}(P_{Y_K \\mid Q} \\| P_{Y_K \\mid P}) \\leq \\prod_{k=1}^K \\eta_k \\cdot D_{KL}(Q \\| P) = \\eta^K \\cdot \\Delta$$\n\nfor homogeneous channels ($\\eta_k = \\eta$). The attacker's influence **decays exponentially** with $K$—each link in the chain further attenuates the perturbation signal. This is iterated application of the data processing inequality.\n\n### Parallel Channels (broadcast)\n\nThe actual multi-judge setting: $X$ is broadcast simultaneously to all $K$ judges.\n\n$$X \\longrightarrow \\begin{cases} W_1 \\to Y_1 \\\\ W_2 \\to Y_2 \\\\ \\vdots \\\\ W_K \\to Y_K \\end{cases}$$\n\nEach channel independently processes the **same input**. The information about the perturbation $P \\to Q$ does not attenuate through a chain; instead, each judge extracts its own view of the perturbation. The total information the attacker injects into the panel **grows** with $K$ (additively, bounded by redundancy), rather than decaying.\n\n**Mathematical distinction**:\n\n| Property | Serial ($X \\to Y_1 \\to \\cdots \\to Y_K$) | Parallel ($X \\to \\{Y_1, \\ldots, Y_K\\}$) |\n|----------|------|----------|\n| SDPI composition | $\\eta_{\\text{joint}} = \\prod_k \\eta_k = \\eta^K$ | $\\eta_{\\text{joint}} \\leq K_{\\text{eff}} \\cdot \\eta_{\\max}$ |\n| Scaling with $K$ | Exponential decay | Linear growth (sublinear with correlation) |\n| Attacker's influence | Vanishes: $\\eta^K \\to 0$ | Bounded: $\\leq K_{\\text{eff}} \\cdot \\eta_{\\max} \\cdot \\Delta$ |\n| Defense mechanism | Information attenuation | Voting concentration |\n\n**The original proposal's error**: treating the multi-judge panel as a serial chain gives a misleadingly optimistic $\\eta^K$ defense. The parallel model is weaker in information attenuation but provides defense through a different mechanism: the combinatorial difficulty of flipping a majority vote.\n\n---\n\n## Lemma 1: Parallel Information Bound\n\n**Lemma 1** (Parallel Channel SDPI). *Let $W^{(K)}: \\mathcal{X} \\to \\mathcal{Y}^K$ be a $K$-judge parallel channel with component SDPI constants $\\eta_1, \\ldots, \\eta_K$ and pairwise mutual information $I(Y_i; Y_j)$ under a reference input distribution. For any two input distributions $P, Q$ over $\\mathcal{X}$:*\n\n$$D_{KL}\\!\\left(W^{(K)} Q \\;\\big\\|\\; W^{(K)} P\\right) \\leq K_{\\text{eff}} \\cdot \\eta_{\\max} \\cdot D_{KL}(Q \\| P)$$\n\n*where*\n\n$$\\boxed{K_{\\text{eff}} = 1 + (K-1) \\cdot \\exp\\!\\left(-2 \\, I_{\\max}\\right)}$$\n\n*with $I_{\\max} = \\max_{i \\neq j} I(Y_i; Y_j)$.*\n\n**Boundary behavior**:\n- $I_{\\max} = 0$ (independent judges): $K_{\\text{eff}} = K$ — full additive composition.\n- $I_{\\max} \\to \\infty$ (perfectly correlated): $K_{\\text{eff}} \\to 1$ — degenerates to a single channel.\n- For fixed $I_{\\max}$: $K_{\\text{eff}}$ is affine in $K$, growing as $\\exp(-2I_{\\max}) \\cdot K$.\n\n---\n\n## Proof Sketch\n\n### Step 1: Individual SDPI\n\nBy definition of the contraction coefficient, for each channel $k$:\n\n$$D_{KL}(W_k Q \\| W_k P) \\leq \\eta_k \\cdot D_{KL}(Q \\| P) \\leq \\eta_{\\max} \\cdot \\Delta$$\n\nwhere $\\Delta = D_{KL}(Q \\| P)$.\n\n### Step 2: Joint channel decomposition\n\nThe joint output KL divergence decomposes via the chain rule:\n\n$$D_{KL}\\!\\left(P_{Y^K \\mid Q} \\;\\big\\|\\; P_{Y^K \\mid P}\\right) = \\sum_{k=1}^K D_{KL}\\!\\left(P_{Y_k \\mid Y_{&lt;k}, Q} \\;\\big\\|\\; P_{Y_k \\mid Y_{&lt;k}, P}\\right)$$\n\nwhere $Y_{&lt;k} = (Y_1, \\ldots, Y_{k-1})$. Each term is the **conditional information increment**: how much new information channel $k$ provides about whether the input was $P$ or $Q$, given the outputs of channels $1, \\ldots, k-1$.\n\n### Step 3: Conditional reduction via correlation\n\nDefine the **residual information ratio** for channel $k$:\n\n$$r_k := \\frac{D_{KL}\\!\\left(P_{Y_k \\mid Y_{&lt;k}, Q} \\;\\big\\|\\; P_{Y_k \\mid Y_{&lt;k}, P}\\right)}{\\eta_{\\max} \\cdot \\Delta}$$\n\nFor the first channel: $r_1 \\leq 1$ (direct SDPI).\n\nFor $k \\geq 2$: conditioning on $Y_{&lt;k}$ partially \"explains\" the variation in $Y_k$ due to the shared input perturbation. When $Y_k$ is correlated with $Y_{&lt;k}$, the previous outputs already carry most of the perturbation signal, reducing $Y_k$'s conditional contribution.\n\nConcretely, in the compound symmetric setting (all pairwise MI equal to $I_0$), the conditional KL for channel $k$ given $Y_{&lt;k}$ reflects the **residual uncertainty** after removing the information already extracted by earlier channels. The Gaussian analysis (Step 4) shows this residual is governed by $\\exp(-2 \\, I_0)$.\n\n### Step 4: Gaussian compound symmetric derivation\n\nConsider the canonical model: $Y_k = X + \\sqrt{\\rho} \\, Z + \\sqrt{1-\\rho} \\, \\varepsilon_k$ where $Z, \\varepsilon_k \\sim \\mathcal{N}(0,1)$ i.i.d., $X \\sim \\mathcal{N}(\\mu, \\sigma_X^2)$, and $\\rho \\in [0,1]$ is the shared-noise fraction.\n\n**Conditional covariance** of $(Y_1, \\ldots, Y_K)$ given $X$:\n\n$$\\Sigma_{ij \\mid X} = \\begin{cases} 1 & i = j \\\\ \\rho & i \\neq j \\end{cases} = (1-\\rho) \\, I + \\rho \\, \\mathbf{1}\\mathbf{1}^\\top$$\n\n**Joint KL divergence** under mean shift $\\mu \\to \\mu'$, $\\Delta\\mu = \\mu' - \\mu$:\n\n$$D_{KL}\\!\\left(P_{Y^K \\mid Q} \\;\\big\\|\\; P_{Y^K \\mid P}\\right) = \\frac{(\\Delta\\mu)^2}{2} \\cdot \\mathbf{1}^\\top \\!\\left[({\\sigma_X^2 + \\rho}) \\, \\mathbf{1}\\mathbf{1}^\\top + (1-\\rho) \\, I\\right]^{-1}\\! \\mathbf{1}$$\n\nBy the Sherman–Morrison formula:\n\n$$\\mathbf{1}^\\top \\Sigma_{\\text{total}}^{-1} \\mathbf{1} = \\frac{K}{(1-\\rho) + K(\\sigma_X^2 + \\rho)}$$\n\nThe **exact** joint SDPI constant:\n\n$$\\eta_{\\text{joint}} = \\frac{K \\, \\sigma_X^2}{(1-\\rho) + K(\\sigma_X^2 + \\rho)}$$\n\nDividing by $\\eta_{\\text{single}} = \\sigma_X^2 / (\\sigma_X^2 + 1)$ gives the **exact** effective channel count:\n\n$$K_{\\text{eff}}^{\\text{exact}} = \\frac{K \\, (\\sigma_X^2 + 1)}{(1-\\rho) + K(\\sigma_X^2 + \\rho)}$$\n\n### Step 5: Deriving the proposed upper bound\n\nIn the Gaussian model, the conditional mutual information:\n\n$$I(Y_i; Y_j \\mid X) = -\\tfrac{1}{2} \\log(1 - \\rho^2) \\implies \\exp(-2 \\, I_0) = 1 - \\rho^2$$\n\nWe claim $K_{\\text{eff}}^{\\text{exact}} \\leq 1 + (K-1)(1-\\rho^2)$, which is the proposed $K_{\\text{eff}}$ when $I_{\\max} = I_0$.\n\n**Proof of the upper bound**: Set $e = \\exp(-2 \\, I_0) = 1 - \\rho^2 \\in [0,1]$ and $a = 1 - e = \\rho^2$. We need:\n\n$$\\frac{K}{1 + (K-1) \\, a \\cdot g(\\sigma_X^2)} \\leq 1 + (K-1)(1-a)$$\n\nwhere $g$ absorbs the SNR dependence. It suffices to show $K \\leq [1 + (K-1)a][1 + (K-1)(1-a)]$.\n\nExpanding the RHS:\n\n$$[1 + (K-1)a][1 + (K-1)(1-a)] = 1 + (K-1) + (K-1)^2 a(1-a) = K + (K-1)^2 a(1-a) \\geq K$$\n\nsince $(K-1)^2 a(1-a) \\geq 0$ for $a \\in [0,1]$, $K \\geq 1$. $\\square$\n\nThe bound is tight at the boundaries: $a = 0$ gives $K_{\\text{eff}} = K$ (independent), $a = 1$ gives $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1$ (fully correlated).\n\n**Remark on the MI quantity**: The above uses $I(Y_i; Y_j \\mid X)$ (conditional on input). When using the unconditional $I(Y_i; Y_j)$—which also includes correlation through the shared signal $X$—the bound remains valid because $I(Y_i; Y_j) \\geq I(Y_i; Y_j \\mid X)$, so $\\exp(-2 \\, I(Y_i; Y_j)) \\leq \\exp(-2 \\, I(Y_i; Y_j \\mid X))$, giving a (possibly looser but still valid) upper bound on $K_{\\text{eff}}$.\n\n---\n\n## Corollary: K_eff Interpretation\n\n### Formula\n\n$$K_{\\text{eff}} = 1 + (K-1) \\cdot \\exp(-2 \\, I_{\\max}) = \\underbrace{\\exp(-2I_{\\max})}_{\\text{independence factor}} \\cdot K + \\underbrace{(1 - \\exp(-2I_{\\max}))}_{\\text{redundancy}}$$\n\n$K_{\\text{eff}}$ decomposes $K$ judges into an \"effective independent count\" that discounts the redundancy from correlated judges.\n\n### Properties\n\n1. **Monotonicity in $I_{\\max}$**: $\\partial K_{\\text{eff}} / \\partial I_{\\max} = -2(K-1) \\exp(-2I_{\\max}) \\leq 0$. More correlation → fewer effective channels.\n\n2. **Affine in $K$**: For fixed $I_{\\max}$, $K_{\\text{eff}} = \\alpha + (1-\\alpha)K$ where $\\alpha = 1 - \\exp(-2I_{\\max}) \\in [0,1]$. Adding a judge increases $K_{\\text{eff}}$ by $\\exp(-2I_{\\max}) < 1$: diminishing returns under correlation.\n\n3. **Marginal gain of diversity**: $\\partial K_{\\text{eff}} / \\partial I_{\\max} \\big|_{I_{\\max}=0}$ = $-2(K-1)$. Near-independence amplifies K strongly; reducing $I_{\\max}$ from small values has disproportionate benefit.\n\n4. **Compound symmetric upper bound**: $K_{\\text{eff}} \\geq K_{\\text{eff}}^{\\text{exact}}$ (the true effective count from the Gaussian model). The gap is $O((K-1)^2 a(1-a))$ where $a = 1 - \\exp(-2I_{\\max})$, maximized at $a = 1/2$ ($I_{\\max} = \\frac{1}{2}\\ln 2 \\approx 0.35$).\n\n### Numerical examples\n\n| $K$ | $I_{\\max}$ | $\\exp(-2I_{\\max})$ | $K_{\\text{eff}}$ | Interpretation |\n|-----|-----------|-------------------|-----------------|---------------|\n| 5   | 0         | 1.0               | 5.0             | Fully independent |\n| 5   | 0.35      | 0.50              | 3.0             | Moderate correlation |\n| 5   | 1.0       | 0.14              | 1.5             | High correlation |\n| 5   | 2.0       | 0.018             | 1.07            | Near-clones |\n| 10  | 0.35      | 0.50              | 5.5             | 10 judges ≈ 5.5 effective |\n| 20  | 0.35      | 0.50              | 10.5            | Half efficiency |\n\n---\n\n## Connection to Manipulation Resistance\n\n### From information bound to attack cost\n\nThe parallel SDPI bound limits the total distinguishability the attacker can create at the panel's output:\n\n$$D_{KL}\\!\\left(W^{(K)} Q \\;\\big\\|\\; W^{(K)} P\\right) \\leq K_{\\text{eff}} \\cdot \\eta_{\\max} \\cdot \\Delta$$\n\nBy Pinsker's inequality, the total variation between attacked and honest output distributions:\n\n$$\\text{TV}\\!\\left(W^{(K)} Q, \\, W^{(K)} P\\right) \\leq \\sqrt{\\frac{K_{\\text{eff}} \\cdot \\eta_{\\max} \\cdot \\Delta}{2}}$$\n\nFor the attacker to flip the panel verdict with probability $\\geq 1 - \\delta$, they need $\\text{TV} \\geq 1 - 2\\delta$, giving:\n\n$$\\boxed{\\Delta \\geq \\frac{2(1-2\\delta)^2}{K_{\\text{eff}} \\cdot \\eta_{\\max}}}$$\n\n### The defense gap: additive information vs. exponential concentration\n\nThe information bound alone gives a **constant** (in $K$) lower bound on the perturbation cost — not an improving defense. The true defense emerges from the interaction between the SDPI bound and the **concentration of the majority vote**.\n\n**Majority vote concentration**: For $K_{\\text{eff}}$ effectively independent judges each with accuracy $p > 1/2$, by Hoeffding's inequality:\n\n$$\\Pr[\\text{incorrect verdict}] \\leq \\exp\\!\\left(-2 K_{\\text{eff}} \\left(p - \\tfrac{1}{2}\\right)^2\\right)$$\n\n**Under attack**: The attacker's perturbation shifts each judge's accuracy from $p$ to at most $p' = p - \\text{TV}(W_k Q, W_k P)$. By Pinsker applied per-channel:\n\n$$p' \\geq p - \\sqrt{\\frac{\\eta_{\\max} \\cdot \\Delta}{2}}$$\n\nFor the verdict to flip **reliably** against this concentration, the attacker needs $p' < 1/2$, AND the majority vote of $K_{\\text{eff}}$ independent coins at rate $p'$ must produce a wrong answer. This requires:\n\n$$\\sqrt{\\frac{\\eta_{\\max} \\cdot \\Delta}{2}} > \\left(p - \\frac{1}{2}\\right) - O\\!\\left(\\frac{1}{\\sqrt{K_{\\text{eff}}}}\\right)$$\n\nThe $O(1/\\sqrt{K_{\\text{eff}}})$ correction arises from the concentration width. The defense improves with $K_{\\text{eff}}$ through the **concentration term**, not through the information bound alone.\n\n### Summary of the defense argument\n\n1. **SDPI**: Each judge's output shift is bounded by $\\eta_{\\max} \\cdot \\Delta$ (contraction).\n2. **Parallel composition**: The total panel information gain is $\\leq K_{\\text{eff}} \\cdot \\eta_{\\max} \\cdot \\Delta$ (sublinear due to correlation).\n3. **Voting concentration**: Flipping the majority of $K_{\\text{eff}}$ independent votes requires the perturbation to clear the accuracy margin **plus** the concentration width $\\Omega(1/\\sqrt{K_{\\text{eff}}})$.\n4. **Manipulation cost**: $\\Delta = \\Omega\\!\\left(\\frac{(p - 1/2)^2}{\\eta_{\\max}}\\right)$, with the success probability of sub-threshold attacks decaying as $\\exp(-\\Omega(K_{\\text{eff}}))$.\n\nThe parallel setting gives a **polynomial** (not exponential) information gain for the attacker, but the majority vote's exponential concentration creates the defense gap.\n\n---\n\n## Open Questions\n\n1. **Tight $K_{\\text{eff}}$ for non-Gaussian channels**: The proposed $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1 + (K-1)\\exp(-2I_{\\max})$ is an upper bound tight for Gaussians. The joint divergence yields η_joint = K·σ_X² / (1-ρ+K(σ_X²+ρ)), which means the effective number of channels is K_eff^exact = K(σ_X²+1) / (1-ρ+K(σ_X²+ρ)). My job is to independently verify experiment conclusions, review MVP design, audit code, check theory derivations, and post findings to the Director via `reviewer_post_to_director`. I operate read-only (no code edits, no experiment execution). The project targets AAAI 2027 (abstract deadline: 2026-07-21). The project is at MVP validation stage with 0 experiments completed. Core hypothesis: K_eff = 1+(K-1)·exp(-2·I_max) predicts manipulation resistance scaling as Ω(K_eff^β).

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **SDPI (Strong Data Processing Inequality)**: Contraction coefficients for noisy channels. Original proposal incorrectly used serial composition (η^K); corrected to parallel (additive, bounded by K_eff·η_max·Δ).
   - **K_eff formula**: 1+(K-1)·exp(-2·I_max) — effective independent channel count from Gaussian copula model. Two distinct formulations exist: (a) effective rank = trace/λ_max = K/[1+(K-1)ρ], (b) MI-based = 1+(K-1)(1-ρ²). These are NOT identical.
   - **PEG (Peer Evaluation Game)**: Chen et al. NeurIPS 2025. DMI-based mechanism with dominant strategy truthfulness. T3 derives degradation curve ε_inc(ρ) = 1-√(1-ρ²) for correlated judges.
   - **Gaussian copula correlation**: ρ parameter — within-family ~0.7-0.8, cross-family ~0.4-0.5.
   - **MVP v3 four-step design**: (1) Correlation measurement with MI + copula ρ, (2) Homo vs hetero panel ASR comparison (core kill criterion), (3) K_eff formula validation, (4) K-scaling analysis with Spearman ρ and log-log regression.
   - **6 models, 3 families**: Qwen (72B, 32B, 14B), Llama (70B, 8B), Mistral (Large). All AWQ quantized. Running on 4× RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell 96GB.
   - **Theory modification track T1-T6**: T1 (SDPI parallel fix), T2 (β as conjecture), T3 (PEG degradation curve), T4 (K* von Neumann entropy), T5 (Asymmetric Duality narrative), T6 (Frontier Diversity Ceiling).
   - **Asymmetric Duality**: Task performance scales exponentially with diversity (Yang et al.), but adversarial robustness scales only polynomially — fundamental asymmetry.

   - **`/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0519_2/projects/cert_manip_resist_eval/src/config.py`**
     - MODEL_REGISTRY: 6 models with paths, GPU IDs, family names. GPU 1 shared (Llama-70B + 8B), GPU 3 shared (Qwen-32B + 14B).
     - HOMO_PANELS: `[["qwen2.5-72b", "qwen2.5-32b"], ["qwen2.5-72b", "qwen2.5-32b", "qwen2.5-14b"]]` (fixed from duplicate bug)
     - HETERO_PANELS: 5 cross-family combinations
     - `VLLM_GPU_MEMORY_UTILIZATION = 0.90` — **still unfixed**, will cause OOM on shared GPUs
     - `VLLM_TEMPERATURE = 0.0`, `N_SAMPLES = 300`, `N_BOOTSTRAP = 1000`

   - **`/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0519_2/projects/cert_manip_resist_eval/src/correlation.py`**
     - `pairwise_mi()`: plug-in estimator with Miller-Madow bias correction ✓
     - `gaussian_copula_rho()`: rank transform → inverse normal → Pearson ✓
     - `bootstrap_ci()`: percentile bootstrap ✓
     - `validate_cross_vs_within()`: mean comparison only, no statistical test (warning flagged)

   - **`/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0519_2/projects/cert_manip_resist_eval/src/keff.py`**
     - `empirical_effective_rank()`: computes trace/λ_max — this is the **wrong** quantity per theory docs (should be MI-based K_eff). **CRITICAL unfixed bug.**
     - `formula_keff()`: computes 1+(K-1)·exp(-2·I_max) — correct per theory.
     - `validate_keff()`: compares formula vs empirical for each panel — will produce misleading results due to formula mismatch.

   - **`/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0519_2/projects/cert_manip_resist_eval/src/attacks.py`**
     - 3 attack classes: PromptInjection (3 templates), SycophancyExploitation, ScoreManipulation (3 variants). 7 total attack instances.
     - `measure_asr()`: conditional ASR (only counts flips where clean verdict was correct) ✓
     - `apply_attack_to_pairs()`: attacks the losing response to try to flip verdict ✓

   - **`/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0519_2/projects/cert_manip_resist_eval/src/panels.py`**
     - `Panel` class with majority voting aggregation.
     - `measure_panel_asr()`: panel-level ASR after majority voting.
     - Tie-breaking: prefers non-"tie" verdict (minor bias, acceptable for MVP).

   - **`/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0519_2/projects/cert_manip_resist_eval/src/scaling.py`**
     - `spearman_correlation()`: K_eff vs ASR ✓
     - `loglog_regression()`: log(ASR) ~ log(K_eff) with bootstrap CI for slope β ✓
     - `run_scaling_analysis()`: full analysis with homo/hetero separation ✓

   - **`/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0519_2/projects/cert_manip_resist_eval/src/judge_service.py`**
     - vLLM-based with AWQ quantization, one model per GPU.
     - Uses tokenizer's chat template when available, fallback to generic format.
     - JSON parsing handles markdown fences.

   - **`/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0519_2/projects/cert_manip_resist_eval/artifacts/theory/t3_peg_degradation.md`**
     - PEG degradation under correlated judges.
     - Theorem 1: ε_inc(ρ) = 1-√(1-ρ²) (incentive sharpness degradation) — proof is sketch, not rigorous.
     - Theorem 2: ε_op(ρ,K) = 1-(K_eff/K)^β (operational degradation).
     - Corollary: K_eff = 1+(K-1)(1-ε_inc)² connects the two.
     - Lemma 2 explicitly notes effective rank ≠ MI-based K_eff.
     - Empirical verification plan V1-V4 with success criteria.
     - Practical threshold: ρ < 0.44 for 90% incentive sharpness.

   - **`/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0519_2/projects/cert_manip_resist_eval/artifacts/theory/t1_sdpi_parallel.md`**
     - Serial→parallel correction: multi-judge is broadcast, not pipeline.
     - Lemma 1: K_eff = 1+(K-1)·exp(-2·I_max) as upper bound for parallel channels.
     - Gaussian proof via Sherman-Morrison, Step 5 upper bound proof.
     - Connection to manipulation resistance: threshold defense + exponential concentration, but does NOT derive K_eff^β scaling. Gap identified.

   - **`/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0519_2/projects/cert_manip_resist_eval/.claude/sessions/reviewer_755cf341/memory/project_bootstrap.md`**
     - Memory file tracking project state at bootstrap.

        "content": "#!/usr/bin/env python3\n# Step 2: Run attack suite on individual judges and panels.\n# Input: Clean eval scores from Step 1, attack definitions.\n# Output: Per-judge and per-panel ASR for each attack type.\n\nimport json\nimport sys\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nsys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent))\n\nfrom src.attacks import ALL_ATTACKS, apply_attack_to_pairs, measure_asr, run_attack_suite\nfrom src.config import ExperimentConfig\nfrom src.eval_pipeline import generate_response_pairs, load_eval_dataset\nfrom src.judge_service import JudgeService\nfrom src.panels import generate_all_panels, measure_all_panels_asr\nfrom src.utils import save_results, set_seed, setup_logging\n\nlogger = setup_logging(\"step2\")\n\n\ndef main():\n    config = ExperimentConfig()\n    set_seed(config.seed)\n\n    all_attack_results = {}\n\n    for ds_name in config.datasets:\n        logger.info(f\"=== Attacks on {ds_name} ===\")\n        samples = load_eval_dataset(ds_name, config.n_samples)\n        pairs = generate_response_pairs(samples, ds_name)\n\n        clean_scores = {}\n        judge_attack_results = {}\n        attacked_scores_by_attack = {attack.name: {} for attack in ALL_ATTACKS}\n\n        # Load each judge sequentially: clean score + all attacks, then unload\n        for model_id in config.model_ids:\n            logger.info(f\"Loading judge {model_id}...\")\n            judge = JudgeService(model_id).load()\n\n            items = [\n                {\"question\": p[\"question\"], \"response_a\": p[\"response_a\"], \"response_b\": p[\"response_b\"]}\n                for p in pairs\n            ]\n            clean_scores[model_id] = judge.batch_score(items)\n\n            logger.info(f\"Running attack suite on {model_id}...\")\n            suite_results = run_attack_suite(judge, pairs, clean_scores[model_id])\n            judge_attack_results[model_id] = suite_results\n\n            for attack in ALL_ATTACKS:\n                attacked_scores_by_attack[attack.name][model_id] = (\n                    suite_results[attack.name][\"attacked_scores\"]\n                )\n\n            judge.unload()\n            logger.info(f\"  {model_id}: GPU memory released\")\n\n        # Panel aggregation (no models needed, just collected scores)\n        panels = generate_all_panels()\n        panel_attack_results = {}\n        for attack in ALL_ATTACKS:\n            panel_asr = measure_all_panels_asr(\n                panels, clean_scores, attacked_scores_by_attack[attack.name], pairs\n            )\n            panel_attack_results[attack.name] = panel_asr\n\n        all_attack_results[ds_name] = {\n            \"judge_attacks\": {\n                mid: {aname: {\"asr\": v[\"asr\"], \"n_flips\": v[\"n_flips\"]} for aname, v in results.items()}\n                for mid, results in judge_attack_results.items()\n            },\n            \"panel_attacks\": panel_attack_results,\n        }\n\n    out = save_results(all_attack_results, config.results_dir, tag=\"step2_attacks\")\n    logger.info(f\"Step 2 complete. Results: {out}\")\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\n",
        "numLines": 83,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 83
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../scripts/run_step2_attacks.py"
  },

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_011wsDcfYkjAdy9Zrv8S1GfT`</summary>

```text
1	# K_eff Formula Validation: compare MI-based empirical K_eff vs. analytical formula.
2	# Formula: K_eff = 1 + (K-1) * exp(-2 * I_max)  [worst-case, uses max pairwise MI]
3	# Empirical: K_eff from all pairwise copula rho   [uses full correlation structure]
4	# Supplementary: von Neumann entropy K* = 2^H(Σ)  [eigenspectrum-based diversity]
5	
6	from typing import Any, Dict, List
7	
8	import numpy as np
9	import pandas as pd
10	
11	from src.utils import setup_logging
12	
13	logger = setup_logging("keff")
14	
15	
16	def empirical_keff_from_rho(rho_matrix: np.ndarray) -> float:
17	    """MI-based empirical K_eff from pairwise copula rho matrix.
18	    K_eff = (1/K) Σ_i [1 + Σ_{j≠i} (1 - ρ_ij²)]
19	    Under Gaussian copula, 1-ρ² = exp(-2·MI), making this consistent
20	    with formula_keff. Difference: empirical uses all pairwise ρ,
21	    formula uses only I_max (worst-case pair)."""
22	    K = rho_matrix.shape[0]
23	    if K <= 1:
24	        return 1.0
25	    total = 0.0
26	    for i in range(K):
27	        k_eff_i = 1.0
28	        for j in range(K):
29	            if j != i:
30	                k_eff_i += 1.0 - rho_matrix[i, j] ** 2
31	        total += k_eff_i
32	    return float(total / K)
33	
34	
35	def von_neumann_kstar(rho_matrix: np.ndarray) -> float:
36	    """Effective diversity via von Neumann entropy: K* = 2^{H(Σ)}.
37	    H(Σ) = -Σ (λ_k / tr(Σ)) · log2(λ_k / tr(Σ)).
38	    Independent validation metric from eigenspectrum."""
39	    eigenvalues = np.linalg.eigvalsh(rho_matrix)
40	    eigenvalues = np.maximum(eigenvalues, 1e-12)
41	    trace = np.sum(eigenvalues)
42	    p = eigenvalues / trace
43	    entropy = -np.sum(p * np.log2(p))
44	    return float(2 ** entropy)
45	
46	
47	def formula_keff(K: int, I_max: float) -> float:
48	    """Analytical K_eff: 1 + (K-1) * exp(-2 * I_max).
49	    K: number of judges in panel.
50	    I_max: maximum pairwise mutual information among panel members."""
51	    return 1.0 + (K - 1) * np.exp(-2.0 * I_max)
52	
53	
54	def build_rho_matrix(
55	    pairwise_df: pd.DataFrame,
56	    judge_ids: List[str],
57	    metric: str = "copula_rho",
58	) -> np.ndarray:
59	    """Build K x K correlation matrix from pairwise DataFrame."""
60	    K = len(judge_ids)
61	    rho = np.eye(K)
62	    id_to_idx = {jid: i for i, jid in enumerate(judge_ids)}
63	
64	    for _, row in pairwise_df.iterrows():
65	        ji, jj = row["judge_i"], row["judge_j"]
66	        if ji in id_to_idx and jj in id_to_idx:
67	            i, j = id_to_idx[ji], id_to_idx[jj]
68	            rho[i, j] = row[metric]
69	            rho[j, i] = row[metric]
70	
71	    return rho
72	
73	
74	def get_max_pairwise_mi(
75	    pairwise_df: pd.DataFrame,
76	    judge_ids: List[str],
77	) -> float:
78	    """Get maximum pairwise MI among a set of judges."""
79	    mask = (
80	        pairwise_df["judge_i"].isin(judge_ids) & pairwise_df["judge_j"].isin(judge_ids)
81	    )
82	    subset = pairwise_df[mask]
83	    if len(subset) == 0:
84	        return 0.0
85	    return float(subset["mi"].max())
86	
87	
88	def validate_keff(
89	    pairwise_df: pd.DataFrame,
90	    panels: List[Dict[str, Any]],
91	) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
92	    """Compare formula K_eff vs empirical effective rank for each panel.
93	    panels: list of dicts with 'judge_ids' key."""
94	    results = []
95	
96	    for panel_info in panels:
97	        judge_ids = panel_info["judge_ids"]
98	        K = len(judge_ids)
99	
100	        # Empirical K_eff: from all pairwise copula rho (MI-based, Option A)
101	        rho_mat = build_rho_matrix(pairwise_df, judge_ids, metric="copula_rho")
102	        k_eff_empirical = empirical_keff_from_rho(rho_mat)
103	
104	        # Formula K_eff: from max pairwise MI (worst-case bound)
105	        I_max = get_max_pairwise_mi(pairwise_df, judge_ids)
106	        k_eff_formula = formula_keff(K, I_max)
107	
108	        # Supplementary: von Neumann entropy K* (Option B)
109	        k_star = von_neumann_kstar(rho_mat)
110	
111	        deviation = abs(k_eff_empirical - k_eff_formula)
112	        rel_deviation = deviation / max(k_eff_empirical, 1e-8)
113	
114	        entry = {
115	            "judge_ids": judge_ids,
116	            "K": K,
117	            "I_max": I_max,
118	            "k_eff_empirical": k_eff_empirical,
119	            "k_eff_formula": k_eff_formula,
120	            "k_star_von_neumann": k_star,
121	            "deviation": deviation,
122	            "relative_deviation": rel_deviation,
123	            "panel_type": panel_info.get("panel_type", "unknown"),
124	        }
125	        results.append(entry)
126	
127	        logger.info(
128	            f"Panel {judge_ids}: K_eff empirical={k_eff_empirical:.3f}, "
129	            f"formula={k_eff_formula:.3f}, K*={k_star:.3f}, deviation={deviation:.3f}"
130	        )
131	
132	    return results
133	
```

      "content": "# K_eff Formula Validation: compare MI-based empirical K_eff vs. analytical formula.\n# Formula: K_eff = 1 + (K-1) * exp(-2 * I_max)  [worst-case, uses max pairwise MI]\n# Empirical: K_eff from all pairwise copula rho   [uses full correlation structure]\n# Supplementary: von Neumann entropy K* = 2^H(Σ)  [eigenspectrum-based diversity]\n\nfrom typing import Any, Dict, List\n\nimport numpy as np\nimport pandas as pd\n\nfrom src.utils import setup_logging\n\nlogger = setup_logging(\"keff\")\n\n\ndef empirical_keff_from_rho(rho_matrix: np.ndarray) -> float:\n    \"\"\"MI-based empirical K_eff from pairwise copula rho matrix.\n    K_eff = (1/K) Σ_i [1 + Σ_{j≠i} (1 - ρ_ij²)]\n    Under Gaussian copula, 1-ρ² = exp(-2·MI), making this consistent\n    with formula_keff. Difference: empirical uses all pairwise ρ,\n    formula uses only I_max (worst-case pair).\"\"\"\n    K = rho_matrix.shape[0]\n    if K <= 1:\n        return 1.0\n    total = 0.0\n    for i in range(K):\n        k_eff_i = 1.0\n        for j in range(K):\n            if j != i:\n                k_eff_i += 1.0 - rho_matrix[i, j] ** 2\n        total += k_eff_i\n    return float(total / K)\n\n\ndef von_neumann_kstar(rho_matrix: np.ndarray) -> float:\n    \"\"\"Effective diversity via von Neumann entropy: K* = 2^{H(Σ)}.\n    H(Σ) = -Σ (λ_k / tr(Σ)) · log2(λ_k / tr(Σ)).\n    Independent validation metric from eigenspectrum.\"\"\"\n    eigenvalues = np.linalg.eigvalsh(rho_matrix)\n    eigenvalues = np.maximum(eigenvalues, 1e-12)\n    trace = np.sum(eigenvalues)\n    p = eigenvalues / trace\n    entropy = -np.sum(p * np.log2(p))\n    return float(2 ** entropy)\n\n\ndef formula_keff(K: int, I_max: float) -> float:\n    \"\"\"Analytical K_eff: 1 + (K-1) * exp(-2 * I_max).\n    K: number of judges in panel.\n    I_max: maximum pairwise mutual information among panel members.\"\"\"\n    return 1.0 + (K - 1) * np.exp(-2.0 * I_max)\n\n\ndef build_rho_matrix(\n    pairwise_df: pd.DataFrame,\n    judge_ids: List[str],\n    metric: str = \"copula_rho\",\n) -> np.ndarray:\n    \"\"\"Build K x K correlation matrix from pairwise DataFrame.\"\"\"\n    K = len(judge_ids)\n    rho = np.eye(K)\n    id_to_idx = {jid: i for i, jid in enumerate(judge_ids)}\n\n    for _, row in pairwise_df.iterrows():\n        ji, jj = row[\"judge_i\"], row[\"judge_j\"]\n        if ji in id_to_idx and jj in id_to_idx:\n            i, j = id_to_idx[ji], id_to_idx[jj]\n            rho[i, j] = row[metric]\n            rho[j, i] = row[metric]\n\n    return rho\n\n\ndef get_max_pairwise_mi(\n    pairwise_df: pd.DataFrame,\n    judge_ids: List[str],\n) -> float:\n    \"\"\"Get maximum pairwise MI among a set of judges.\"\"\"\n    mask = (\n        pairwise_df[\"judge_i\"].isin(judge_ids) & pairwise_df[\"judge_j\"].isin(judge_ids)\n    )\n    subset = pairwise_df[mask]\n    if len(subset) == 0:\n        return 0.0\n    return float(subset[\"mi\"].max())\n\n\ndef validate_keff(\n    pairwise_df: pd.DataFrame,\n    panels: List[Dict[str, Any]],\n) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:\n    \"\"\"Compare formula K_eff vs empirical effective rank for each panel.\n    panels: list of dicts with 'judge_ids' key.\"\"\"\n    results = []\n\n    for panel_info in panels:\n        judge_ids = panel_info[\"judge_ids\"]\n        K = len(judge_ids)\n\n        # Empirical K_eff: from all pairwise copula rho (MI-based, Option A)\n        rho_mat = build_rho_matrix(pairwise_df, judge_ids, metric=\"copula_rho\")\n        k_eff_empirical = empirical_keff_from_rho(rho_mat)\n\n        # Formula K_eff: from max pairwise MI (worst-case bound)\n        I_max = get_max_pairwise_mi(pairwise_df, judge_ids)\n        k_eff_formula = formula_keff(K, I_max)\n\n        # Supplementary: von Neumann entropy K* (Option B)\n        k_star = von_neumann_kstar(rho_mat)\n\n        deviation = abs(k_eff_empirical - k_eff_formula)\n        rel_deviation = deviation / max(k_eff_empirical, 1e-8)\n\n        entry = {\n            \"judge_ids\": judge_ids,\n            \"K\": K,\n            \"I_max\": I_max,\n            \"k_eff_empirical\": k_eff_empirical,\n            \"k_eff_formula\": k_eff_formula,\n            \"k_star_von_neumann\": k_star,\n            \"deviation\": deviation,\n            \"relative_deviation\": rel_deviation,\n            \"panel_type\": panel_info.get(\"panel_type\", \"unknown\"),\n        }\n        results.append(entry)\n\n        logger.info(\n            f\"Panel {judge_ids}: K_eff empirical={k_eff_empirical:.3f}, \"\n            f\"formula={k_eff_formula:.3f}, K*={k_star:.3f}, deviation={deviation:.3f}\"\n        )\n\n    return results\n",
      "numLines": 133,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 133
    }
  },
  "sourceToolAssistantUUID": "3e8a3753-ff64-4453-9c88-38fab469a0fe",

  "cwd": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0519_2/projects/cert_manip_resist_eval/.claude/sessions/reviewer_755cf341",
  "sessionId": "4cb21f02-e354-4562-a24a-1c708312b56f",
  "version": "2.1.126",
  "gitBranch": "HEAD",
  "slug": "soft-sparking-willow"
}

pairwise_df.iterrows():\\n        ji, jj = row[\\\"judge_i\\\"], row[\\\"judge_j\\\"]\\n        if ji in id_to_idx and jj in id_to_idx:\\n            i, j = id_to_idx[ji], id_to_idx[jj]\\n            rho[i, j] = row[metric]\\n            rho[j, i] = row[metric]\\n\\n    return rho\\n\\n\\ndef get_max_pairwise_mi(\\n    pairwise_df: pd.DataFrame,\\n    judge_ids: List[str],\\n) -> float:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Get maximum pairwise MI among a set of judges.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    mask = (\\n        pairwise_df[\\\"judge_i\\\"].isin(judge_ids) & pairwise_df[\\\"judge_j\\\"].isin(judge_ids)\\n    )\\n    subset = pairwise_df[mask]\\n    if len(subset) == 0:\\n        return 0.0\\n    return float(subset[\\\"mi\\\"].max())\\n\\n\\ndef validate_keff(\\n    pairwise_df: pd.DataFrame,\\n    panels: List[Dict[str, Any]],\\n) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Compare formula K_eff vs empirical effective rank for each panel.\\n    panels: list of dicts with 'judge_ids' key.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    results = []\\n\\n    for panel_info in panels:\\n        judge_ids = panel_info[\\\"judge_ids\\\"]\\n        K = len(judge_ids)\\n\\n        # Empirical K_eff: from all pairwise copula rho (MI-based, Option A)\\n        rho_mat = build_rho_matrix(pairwise_df, judge_ids, metric=\\\"copula_rho\\\")\\n        k_eff_empirical = empirical_keff_from_rho(rho_mat)\\n\\n        # Formula K_eff: from max pairwise MI (worst-case bound)\\n        I_max = get_max_pairwise_mi(pairwise_df, judge_ids)\\n        k_eff_formula = formula_keff(K, I_max)\\n\\n        # Supplementary: von Neumann entropy K* (Option B)\\n        k_star = von_neumann_kstar(rho_mat)\\n\\n        deviation = abs(k_eff_empirical - k_eff_formula)\\n        rel_deviation = deviation / max(k_eff_empirical, 1e-8)\\n\\n        entry = {\\n            \\\"judge_ids\\\": judge_ids,\\n            \\\"K\\\": K,\\n            \\\"I_max\\\": I_max,\\n            \\\"k_eff_empirical\\\": k_eff_empirical,\\n            \\\"k_eff_formula\\\": k_eff_formula,\\n            \\\"k_star_von_neumann\\\": k_star,\\n            \\\"deviation\\\": deviation,\\n            \\\"relative_deviation\\\": rel_deviation,\\n            \\\"panel_type\\\": panel_info.get(\\\"panel_type\\\", \\\"unknown\\\"),\\n        }\\n        results.append(entry)\\n\\n        logger.info(\\n            f\\\"Panel {judge_ids}: K_eff empirical={k_eff_empirical:.3f}, \\\"\\n            f\\\"formula={k_eff_formula:.3f}, K*={k_star:.3f}, deviation={deviation:.3f}\\\"\\n        )\\n\\n    return results\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", <details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
The keff.py has been significantly rewritten. The old `empirical_effective_rank()` function (which computed trace/λ_max) has been replaced with `empirical_keff_from_rho()` which computes the MI-based K_eff using the formula:

K_eff = (1/K) Σ_i [1 + Σ_{j≠i} (1 - ρ_ij²)]

This is a much better approach. Under Gaussian copula, 1-ρ² = exp(-2·MI), so this is indeed the MI-based K_eff. 

Now I see the key distinction: `empirical_keff_from_rho` leverages the complete correlation structure across all pairs, while `formula_keff` only considers the maximum MI as a worst-case scenario. This means the formula version provides a lower bound on the effective K, since using the worst-case pair correlation naturally minimizes the resulting K_eff value.

The sync-keff output confirms this relationship—empirical comes in at 1.750, the formula bound at 1.549, and the von Neumann entropy metric at 1.755, which validates the empirical approach. The critical fix was replacing the old trace-based rank calculation with the MI-based empirical method, and adding the entropy-based K* as an independent check.

Now I need to verify the mathematical soundness of `empirical_keff_from_rho` and ensure the implementation is correct. 2. Whether `validate_keff` correctly interprets the deviation between empirical and formula

Let me think about the math:

For the empirical K_eff from copula rho:
K_eff = (1/K) Σ_i [1 + Σ_{j≠i} (1 - ρ_ij²)]

For judge i, the "effective independence" is:
k_eff_i = 1 + Σ_{j≠i} (1 - ρ_ij²)

If all ρ_ij = 0 (independent), then k_eff_i = 1 + (K-1)·1 = K, so K_eff = K. ✓
If all ρ_ij = 1 (perfect correlation), then k_eff_i = 1 + 0 = 1, so K_eff = 1. ✓

This formula captures the average effective number of independent information sources per judge, and under Gaussian copula the relationship 1-ρ² = exp(-2·MI_ij) ties it directly to mutual information. The simplified formula K_eff = 1 + (K-1)·exp(-2·I_max) uses worst-case correlation as a lower bound, which is mathematically sound. The docstring is slightly misleading though—it mentions comparing against effective rank when it's actually comparing two different K_eff calculations, and the deviation metric measures the gap between the worst-case bound and the empirical value.

Now I should check the gpu_memory_utilization fix in config.py and verify the model download status.

Load model\\n    logger.info(\\\"Loading Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct-AWQ on cuda:3...\\\")\\n    judge = JudgeService(\\\"qwen2.5-14b\\\")\\n    judge.load()\\n    t_load = time.time() - t0\\n    logger.info(f\\\"Model loaded in {t_load:.1f}s\\\")\\n    \\n    # 2. Load dataset (30 samples)\\n    logger.info(\\\"Loading MMLU (30 samples)...\\\")\\n    os.environ.setdefault(\\\"HF_HOME\\\", \\\"/root/autodl-tmp/.hf_cache\\\")\\n    samples = load_eval_dataset(\\\"mmlu\\\", 30)\\n    pairs = generate_response_pairs(samples, \\\"mmlu\\\")\\n    logger.info(f\\\"Generated {len(pairs)} pairs\\\")\\n    \\n    # 3. Pairwise scoring\\n    logger.info(\\\"Running pairwise scoring...\\\")\\n    items = [\\n        {\\\"question\\\": p[\\\"question\\\"], \\\"response_a\\\": p[\\\"response_a\\\"], \\\"response_b\\\": p[\\\"response_b\\\"]}\\n        for p in pairs\\n    ]\\n    t1 = time.time()\\n    scores = judge.batch_score(items, mode=\\\"pairwise\\\")\\n    t_infer = time.time() - t1\\n    \\n    # 4. Analyze results\\n    n_valid = sum(1 for s in scores if \\\"winner\\\" in s and s[\\\"winner\\\"] in (\\\"A\\\", \\\"B\\\", \\\"tie\\\"))\\n    n_error = sum(1 for s in scores if \\\"error\\\" in s)\\n    winners = {\\\"A\\\": 0, \\\"B\\\": 0, \\\"tie\\\": 0}\\n    for s in scores:\\n        w = s.get(\\\"winner\\\")\\n        if w in winners:\\n            winners[w] += 1\\n    \\n    print(f\\\"\\\\n{'='*50}\\\")\\n    print(f\\\"INFERENCE TEST RESULTS\\\")\\n    print(f\\\"{'='*50}\\\")\\n    print(f\\\"Model: qwen2.5-14b\\\")\\n    print(f\\\"Load time: {t_load:.1f}s\\\")\\n    print(f\\\"Inference time: {t_infer:.1f}s ({len(items)} pairs)\\\")\\n    print(f\\\"Throughput: {len(items)/t_infer:.1f} pairs/sec\\\")\\n    print(f\\\"Valid: {n_valid}/{len(scores)} ({100*n_valid/len(scores):.0f}%)\\\")\\n    print(f\\\"Parse errors: {n_error}\\\")\\n    print(f\\\"Winners: A={winners['A']}, B={winners['B']}, tie={winners['tie']}\\\")\\n    \\n    # Print 3 example outputs\\n    print(f\\\"\\\\n--- Sample outputs ---\\\")\\n    for i, s in enumerate(scores[:3]):\\n        print(f\\\"  [{i}] winner={s.get('winner','N/A')}, score_a={s.get('score_a','N/A')}, score_b={s.get('score_b','N/A')}\\\")\\n        if 'reasoning' in s:\\n            print(f\\\"       reasoning: {s['reasoning'][:100]}...\\\")\\n    \\n    # 5. Likert scoring (5 samples)\\n    logger.info(\\\"Running Likert scoring (5 samples)...\\\")\\n    likert_scores = []\\n    for p in pairs[:5]:\\n        ls = judge.score_likert(p[\\\"question\\\"], p[\\\"response_a\\\"])\\n        likert_scores.append(ls)\\n    \\n    n_likert_valid = sum(1 for s in likert_scores if \\\"score\\\" in s)\\n    print(f\\\"\\\\nLikert valid: {n_likert_valid}/5\\\")\\n    for i, s in enumerate(likert_scores):\\n        print(f\\\"  [{i}] score={s.get('score','N/A')}\\\")\\n    \\n    # 6. Unload\\n    logger.info(\\\"Unloading model...\\\")\\n    judge.unload()\\n    t_total = time.time() - t0\\n    logger.info(f\\\"Unload complete. Total time: {t_total:.1f}s\\\")\\n    \\n    # 7. Verdict\\n    if n_valid >= len(scores) * 0.5:\\n        print(f\\\"\\\\n✅ INFERENCE CHAIN TEST PASSED (total {t_total:.1f}s)\\\")\\n        return 0\\n    else:\\n        print(f\\\"\\\\n❌ INFERENCE CHAIN TEST FAILED: too many parse errors ({n_error}/{len(scores)})\\\")\\n        return 1\\n\\nif __name__ == \\\"__main__\\\":\\n    sys.exit(main())\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.42}}"}

   - GPU 1: 3 MiB (idle)
   - GPU 2: 3 MiB (idle)
   - GPU 3: 88267 MiB / 97887 MiB (90% — Qwen-14B loaded via vLLM with gpu_memory_utilization=0.90)

3. **Important observation about gpu_memory_utilization**: Qwen-14B is only ~9.4GB but vLLM with 0.90 utilization allocated **88GB** on GPU 3. This is a critical issue — when the agent tries to load Qwen-32B on the same GPU, there won't be enough memory since 88GB is already occupied.

But looking at the test script, it loads and unloads models sequentially with `judge.unload()` at the end, so they never coexist. Let me check if the actual experiment pipeline has the same pattern. Examining run_step2_attacks.py more carefully, it also loads judges sequentially — each model gets loaded, scored, attacked, then unloaded before moving to the next one. So they shouldn't actually conflict on the same GPU either.

The real issue is that vLLM at 0.90 utilization pre-allocates ~88GB of GPU memory for KV cache regardless of the model's actual size. When a small model like Qwen-14B (~9.4GB weights) loads, vLLM still reserves that large KV cache. Since the pipeline loads one model at a time and unloads before the next, 0.90 utilization is actually fine for each individual model — the memory gets released between loads. beneficial — more KV cache = faster batch inference). My warning still stands for any scenario where two models need to be loaded simultaneously, but the current scripts don't do that.

Let me reconsider: is 0.90 actually a problem? For sequential loading:
- Load Qwen-72B on GPU 0: 0.90 × 97887 ≈ 88GB. Model weights ~37GB AWQ, leaves ~51GB for KV cache. Fine.
- Unload, then load Llama-70B on GPU 1: Same, fine.
- Each model loaded and unloaded in turn.

For GPU-sharing models:
- GPU 1: Llama-70B (~35GB) then Llama-8B (~5GB) — sequential, fine.
- GPU 3: Qwen-32B (~21GB) then Qwen-14B (~9.4GB) — sequential, fine.

With sequential load/unload, 0.90 is actually fine. I was worried about simultaneous loading, but that's not what's happening here. Let me check if Step 1 loads multiple models at once or if it also works sequentially like Step 2. Since I don't have the Step 1 script visible, I'm assuming it follows the same pattern of computing pairwise correlations from saved scores rather than loading everything together. So my real concern is whether any script actually loads multiple models simultaneously on the same GPU — if they all work sequentially, then 0.90 is fine and even beneficial for KV cache.

The critical fix is that `empirical_keff_from_rho()` now correctly computes MI-based K_eff from copula ρ, which was the major bug I flagged. The Qwen-14B smoke test is running on GPU 3 with CUDA graph capture done, currently processing 30 pairwise scoring items. Model downloads are mostly complete: Qwen-32B and Qwen-14B are fully downloaded, Qwen-72B and Mistral-Large are still in progress, and Llama-70B hasn't started yet.

GPU memory is no longer a concern since sequential loading keeps utilization at 0.90. Let me verify the K_eff calculation is working correctly by checking the formula for a two-judge case where the correlation ρ should yield K_eff = 2 - ρ² for each judge.

The empirical method and the Gaussian copula formula both converge to the same result for K=2, but with three judges and heterogeneous correlations, the empirical approach accounts for all pairwise relationships while the formula only uses the maximum, so empirical should be at least as large.

The worker test showed empirical=1.750 versus formula=1.549, confirming this divergence comes from the heterogeneous correlation structure. Now I need to double-check the docstring formula and verify it against the theoretical calculation—for K=3 with ρ=0.5, the uniform formula gives K_eff = 2.5, so I should compare that to what the new approach produces.

I'm realizing the formula and empirical K_eff are measuring different things: the formula gives a worst-case bound using maximum correlation, while the empirical version averages across all pairwise correlations. So the deviation between them isn't necessarily a validation failure—it's expected because they're answering different questions about effective sample size.

The real test comes in MVP Step 4, where I check if K_eff actually predicts manipulation resistance scaling through Spearman correlation and log-log regression. For Step 3, comparing empirical versus formula is still valuable because it tells me whether one dominant pair drives the effect or if there's heterogeneous correlation across the dataset. The code structure handles this analysis correctly, and resolving the K_eff calculation removes my main concern. Now I should finalize my review and check on the inference test.

Excellent! The inference test passed successfully. Here's the complete picture:

**Inference test results (Qwen-14B):**
- Load time: 56.1s
- 30 pairwise pairs processed in 47.9s (0.6 pairs/sec)
- Valid: 26/30 (87%) — 4 responses didn't have "winner" field but no parse errors
- Winners: A=5, B=21, tie=0 — heavy B bias, which is interesting
- Likert scoring: 5/5 valid, scores: 3, 3, 2, 2, 5


- Total time: 153.9s
- ✅ PASSED

The B bias is striking — 21 out of 26 valid responses favored B, which suggests potential position bias. Since the test uses MMLU with one correct and one incorrect response per pair, I need to verify how the pairing assigns which answer gets which position. If the correct answer consistently maps to B, that's a confound that could skew the attack success rate measurements.

For this smoke test, the judge is functioning adequately at 87% validity, but I should note that 4 invalid responses (missing the "winner" field) would scale to roughly 39 lost samples in a full 300-sample experiment. The Likert scores are also quite low at 2, 2, 3, which warrants investigation. Position bias should be controlled in the actual experiment by randomizing A/B positions, but for now this baseline is acceptable.

Now I'm checking the Llama-70B download status — there's a temp file still present but the download process isn't running, which could mean either the shard completed successfully and ModelScope didn't clean up, or the download was interrupted partway through. I need to check the file size to determine which scenario occurred.
```

1	# Judge Evaluation Pipeline.
2	# Loads datasets, generates response pairs, collects judge scores.
3	# Output: scores[judge_id][sample_id] = {winner, score_a, score_b}
4	
5	import json
6	import random
7	from pathlib import Path
8	from typing import Any, Dict, List, Tuple
9	
10	from datasets import load_dataset
11	from tqdm import tqdm
12	
13	from src.config import DATASET_REGISTRY, ExperimentConfig
14	from src.judge_service import JudgeService
15	from src.utils import save_results, setup_logging, set_seed
16	
17	logger = setup_logging("eval_pipeline")
18	
19	
20	def load_eval_dataset(dataset_name: str, n_samples: int) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
21	    """Load and sample from a HuggingFace dataset."""
22	    hf_id, config_name, split, default_n = DATASET_REGISTRY[dataset_name]
23	    n = min(n_samples, default_n)
24	    ds = load_dataset(hf_id, config_name, split=split)
25	    indices = random.sample(range(len(ds)), min(n, len(ds)))
26	    samples = [ds[i] for i in indices]
27	    logger.info(f"Loaded {len(samples)} samples from {dataset_name}")
28	    return samples
29	
30	
31	def _format_mmlu_question(sample: Dict) -> str:
32	    choices = sample["choices"]
33	    q = sample["question"]
34	    options = "\n".join(f"  ({chr(65+i)}) {c}" for i, c in enumerate(choices))
35	    return f"{q}\n{options}"
36	
37	
38	def _format_arc_question(sample: Dict) -> str:
39	    choices = sample["choices"]
40	    q = sample["question"]
41	    labels = choices["label"]
42	    texts = choices["text"]
43	    options = "\n".join(f"  ({l}) {t}" for l, t in zip(labels, texts))
44	    return f"{q}\n{options}"
45	
46	
47	def _generate_good_response(sample: Dict, dataset_name: str) -> str:
48	    """Generate a correct response with explanation."""
49	    if dataset_name == "mmlu":
50	        answer_idx = sample["answer"]
51	        answer_letter = chr(65 + answer_idx)
52	        answer_text = sample["choices"][answer_idx]
53	        return (
54	            f"The correct answer is ({answer_letter}) {answer_text}. "
55	            f"This is because the question asks about a concept where "
56	            f"option {answer_letter} is the most accurate and complete choice "
57	            f"among the given alternatives."
58	        )
59	    else:  # arc_challenge
60	        answer_key = sample["answerKey"]
61	        labels = sample["choices"]["label"]
62	        texts = sample["choices"]["text"]
63	        idx = labels.index(answer_key)
64	        answer_text = texts[idx]
65	        return (
66	            f"The correct answer is ({answer_key}) {answer_text}. "
67	            f"This answer is correct because it accurately addresses "
68	            f"the scientific concept being tested in the question."
69	        )
70	
71	
72	def _generate_bad_response(sample: Dict, dataset_name: str) -> str:
73	    """Generate an incorrect response with plausible-sounding but wrong reasoning."""
74	    if dataset_name == "mmlu":
75	        answer_idx = sample["answer"]
76	        choices = sample["choices"]
77	        wrong_indices = [i for i in range(len(choices)) if i != answer_idx]
78	        wrong_idx = random.choice(wrong_indices)
79	        wrong_letter = chr(65 + wrong_idx)
80	        wrong_text = choices[wrong_idx]
81	        return (
82	            f"The answer is ({wrong_letter}) {wrong_text}. "
83	            f"While other options might seem plausible, this option "
84	            f"best captures the key aspect of the question."
85	        )
86	    else:  # arc_challenge
87	        answer_key = sample["answerKey"]
88	        labels = sample["choices"]["label"]
89	        texts = sample["choices"]["text"]
90	        wrong_labels = [l for l in labels if l != answer_key]
91	        wrong_label = random.choice(wrong_labels)
92	        wrong_idx = labels.index(wrong_label)
93	        wrong_text = texts[wrong_idx]
94	        return (
95	            f"The answer is ({wrong_label}) {wrong_text}. "
96	            f"This is the most scientifically accurate choice "
97	            f"given the context of the question."
98	        )
99	
100	
101	def generate_response_pairs(
102	    samples: List[Dict], dataset_name: str
103	) -> List[Dict[str, str]]:
104	    """Generate (question, good_response, bad_response) triples.
105	    Randomly assign good/bad to A/B positions to avoid position bias."""
106	    pairs = []
107	    for sample in samples:
108	        if dataset_name == "mmlu":
109	            question = _format_mmlu_question(sample)
110	        else:
111	            question = _format_arc_question(sample)
112	
113	        good = _generate_good_response(sample, dataset_name)
114	        bad = _generate_bad_response(sample, dataset_name)
115	
116	        # Randomly assign to A/B to control for position bias
117	        if random.random() < 0.5:
118	            pairs.append({
119	                "question": question,
120	                "response_a": good,
121	                "response_b": bad,
122	                "ground_truth_winner": "A",
123	            })
124	        else:
125	            pairs.append({
126	                "question": question,
127	                "response_a": bad,
128	                "response_b": good,
129	                "ground_truth_winner": "B",
130	            })
131	    return pairs
132	
133	
134	def run_evaluation(
135	    model_ids: List[str],
136	    pairs: List[Dict[str, str]],
137	    dataset_name: str,
138	) -> Dict[str, List[Dict[str, Any]]]:
139	    """Run all judges on all pairs. Loads/unloads each judge sequentially
140	    to avoid GPU memory conflicts when models share GPUs."""
141	    all_scores: Dict[str, List[Dict[str, Any]]] = {}
142	
143	    items = [
144	        {"question": p["question"], "response_a": p["response_a"], "response_b": p["response_b"]}
145	        for p in pairs
146	    ]
147	
148	    for model_id in model_ids:
149	        logger.info(f"Loading judge {model_id}...")
150	        judge = JudgeService(model_id)
151	        judge.load()
152	
153	        logger.info(f"Scoring {len(pairs)} pairs ({dataset_name})")
154	        scores = judge.batch_score(items, mode="pairwise")
155	
156	        for score, pair in zip(scores, pairs):
157	            score["ground_truth_winner"] = pair["ground_truth_winner"]
158	            score["dataset"] = dataset_name
159	
160	        all_scores[model_id] = scores
161	        logger.info(f"  {model_id}: {len(scores)} scores collected")
162	
163	        judge.unload()
164	        logger.info(f"  {model_id}: GPU memory released")
165	
166	    return all_scores
167	
168	
169	def run_full_pipeline(
170	    model_ids: List[str],
171	    config: ExperimentConfig,
172	) -> Dict[str, Any]:
173	    """Run evaluation across all configured datasets.
174	    Each judge is loaded/unloaded sequentially to support GPU sharing."""
175	    set_seed(config.seed)
176	    all_results: Dict[str, Any] = {}
177	
178	    for ds_name in config.datasets:
179	        logger.info(f"=== Dataset: {ds_name} ===")
180	        samples = load_eval_dataset(ds_name, config.n_samples)
181	        pairs = generate_response_pairs(samples, ds_name)
182	        scores = run_evaluation(model_ids, pairs, ds_name)
183	        all_results[ds_name] = {
184	            "scores": scores,
185	            "n_pairs": len(pairs),
186	            "pairs": pairs,
187	        }
188	
189	    out_path = save_results(all_results, config.results_dir, tag="eval_pipeline")
190	    logger.info(f"Results saved to {out_path}")
191	    return all_results
192	
```

Loads/unloads each judge sequentially\n    to avoid GPU memory conflicts when models share GPUs.\"\"\"\n    all_scores: Dict[str, List[Dict[str, Any]]] = {}\n\n    items = [\n        {\"question\": p[\"question\"], \"response_a\": p[\"response_a\"], \"response_b\": p[\"response_b\"]}\n        for p in pairs\n    ]\n\n    for model_id in model_ids:\n        logger.info(f\"Loading judge {model_id}...\")\n        judge = JudgeService(model_id)\n        judge.load()\n\n        logger.info(f\"Scoring {len(pairs)} pairs ({dataset_name})\")\n        scores = judge.batch_score(items, mode=\"pairwise\")\n\n        for score, pair in zip(scores, pairs):\n            score[\"ground_truth_winner\"] = pair[\"ground_truth_winner\"]\n            score[\"dataset\"] = dataset_name\n\n        all_scores[model_id] = scores\n        logger.info(f\"  {model_id}: {len(scores)} scores collected\")\n\n        judge.unload()\n        logger.info(f\"  {model_id}: GPU memory released\")\n\n    return all_scores\n\n\ndef run_full_pipeline(\n    model_ids: List[str],\n    config: ExperimentConfig,\n) -> Dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Run evaluation across all configured datasets.\n    Each judge is loaded/unloaded sequentially to support GPU sharing.\"\"\"\n    set_seed(config.seed)\n    all_results: Dict[str, Any] = {}\n\n    for ds_name in config.datasets:\n        logger.info(f\"=== Dataset: {ds_name} ===\")\n        samples = load_eval_dataset(ds_name, config.n_samples)\n        pairs = generate_response_pairs(samples, ds_name)\n        scores = run_evaluation(model_ids, pairs, ds_name)\n        all_results[ds_name] = {\n            \"scores\": scores,\n            \"n_pairs\": len(pairs),\n            \"pairs\": pairs,\n        }\n\n    out_path = save_results(all_results, config.results_dir, tag=\"eval_pipeline\")\n    logger.info(f\"Results saved to {out_path}\")\n    return all_results\n",
      "numLines": 192,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 192
    }
  },

K_eff 代码修复已验证 ✅（原 CRITICAL → 已解决）\n\n`src/keff.py` 已重写。旧的 `empirical_effective_rank()`（trace/λ_max，有效秩）替换为 `empirical_keff_from_rho()`（MI-based K_eff）：\n\n```\nK_eff = (1/K) Σ_i [1 + Σ_{j≠i} (1 - ρ_ij²)]\n```\n\n数学验证：\n- K=2, ρ=ρ₁₂ → K_eff = 2-ρ² = formula K_eff（一致）✓\n- K=3, 均匀 ρ=0.5 → K_eff = 2.5 = T3 理论值 ✓\n- 独立时 K_eff=K，完全相关时 K_eff=1 ✓\n\n`formula_keff` 用 I_max（最差对），是 empirical 的下界。sync-keff worker 的测试值 empirical=1.750 > formula=1.549 符合预期。\n\n新增 `von_neumann_kstar()`（K*=2^H(Σ)）作为独立验证指标，值 1.755 ≈ empirical，交叉验证通过。\n\n**小问题**：`validate_keff` 第92行 docstring 仍写 \"empirical effective rank\"，应改为 \"empirical K_eff\"。不阻塞。\n\n## 2. 推理测试通过 ✅\n\nQwen-14B on cuda:3, MMLU 30样本：\n- 加载 56.1s，推理 47.9s，总计 153.9s\n- 有效率 26/30 (87%)，无 parse error\n- Pairwise + Likert 均正常工作\n- 位置偏差已控制：`generate_response_pairs()` 随机分配 A/B 位置 ✓\n\n## 3. gpu_memory_utilization=0.90 降级为 INFO\n\n之前标 WARNING × 3 的问题经代码审查后降级。`run_evaluation()` 和 `run_step2_attacks.py` 都是**顺序加载**（load → score → unload → next），不存在同 GPU 双模型共存的场景。0.90 对单模型反而有利（更大 KV cache）。\n\n## 4. {

  "content": "---\nname: Project bootstrap state\ndescription: cert_manip_resist_eval 项目状态——MVP v3 设计、代码审查结果、模型下载、推理验证\ntype: project\n---\n\n项目 2026-05-21 立项，MVP v3 阶段，registry 0 实验。\n\n**MVP v3 四步验证**: (1)相关性测量(MI+copula ρ) → (2)Homo vs Hetero ASR → (3)K_eff公式验证 → (4)K-scaling分析。6 模型 3 家族，本地 4×RTX PRO 6000 96GB。\n\n**代码审查结果 (2026-05-21~22)**:\n- ✅ K_eff CRITICAL bug 已修复：`empirical_effective_rank()`→`empirical_keff_from_rho()`，MI-based 公式验证通过\n- ✅ HOMO_PANELS 重复模型 bug 已修复\n- ✅ 推理测试通过：Qwen-14B, 87% valid, pairwise+Likert 正常\n- ✅ 位置偏差控制：随机 A/B 分配\n- ✅ gpu_memory_utilization=0.90：顺序加载无问题，降级为 INFO\n- ⚠️ Llama-70B shard 5 下载失败，需重试\n- ⏳ Qwen-72B, Mistral-Large 下载进行中\n\n**理论审查**:\n- T1 SDPI 并联修正数学正确，但未完成 SDPI→Ω(K_eff^β) 的推导链\n- T3 PEG degradation 数学正确，Theorem 1 实为 sketch 非严格证明\n- K_eff code vs theory 不一致已解决\n\n**投稿**: AAAI 2027 abstract 2026-07-21\n\n**Why:** MVP 执行前代码和理论的基础审查已完成，关键 bug 已修复。\n**How to apply:** 后续关注模型下载完成 → smoke test → MVP Step 1 执行。\n"
}
```

          "-**外审**: FAILED (Z=35%)，核心修正需求：SDPI 并联通道组合逻辑（串联→并联）、½ 指数作 conjecture + 经验估计、protocol-aware adaptive attacks。",
          "+**MVP v3 四步验证**: (1)相关性测量(MI+copula ρ) → (2)Homo vs Hetero ASR → (3)K_eff公式验证 → (4)K-scaling分析。6 模型 3 家族，本地 4×RTX PRO 6000 96GB。",
          " ",
          "-**MVP 三步验证**: 相关性测量 → 攻击抗性 scaling → K_eff 回归。API 模型，~$50-200。",
          "+**代码审查结果 (2026-05-21~22)**:",
          "+- ✅ K_eff CRITICAL bug 已修复：`empirical_effective_rank()`→`empirical_keff_from_rho()`，MI-based 公式验证通过",
          "+- ✅ HOMO_PANELS 重复模型 bug 已修复",
          "+- ✅ 推理测试通过：Qwen-14B, 87% valid, pairwise+Likert 正常",
          "+- ✅ 位置偏差控制：随机 A/B 分配",
          "+- ✅ gpu_memory_utilization=0.90：顺序加载无问题，降级为 INFO",
          "+- ⚠️ Llama-70B shard 5 下载失败，需重试",
          "+- ⏳ Qwen-72B, Mistral-Large 下载进行中",
          " ",
          "-**首次审查发现（2026-05-21）**: 4 个 warning——Step 3 统计功效不足（n=3）、缺 homo/hetero panel 对照、kill criteria 操作定义模糊、K_eff 公式本身未验证。已提交 Director。",
          "+**理论审查**:",
          "+- T1 SDPI 并联修正数学正确，但未完成 SDPI→Ω(K_eff^β) 的推导链",
          "+- T3 PEG degradation 数学正确，Theorem 1 实为 sketch 非严格证明",
          "+- K_eff code vs theory 不一致已解决",
          " ",
          "-**投稿目标**: AAAI 2027 abstract 2026-07-21, full 2026-07-28（~2 个月）。",
          "+**投稿**: AAAI 2027 abstract 2026-07-21",

    "originalFile": "---\nname: Project bootstrap state\ndescription: cert_manip_resist_eval 立项时的关键状态——MVP 设计、外审结果、投稿目标、首次审查发现\ntype: project\n---\n\n项目 2026-05-21 立项，MVP 阶段，registry 0 实验。\n\n**外审**: FAILED (Z=35%)，核心修正需求：SDPI 并联通道组合逻辑（串联→并联）、½ 指数作 conjecture + 经验估计、protocol-aware adaptive attacks。\n\n**MVP 三步验证**: 相关性测量 → 攻击抗性 scaling → K_eff 回归。API 模型，~$50-200。\n\n**首次审查发现（2026-05-21）**: 4 个 warning——Step 3 统计功效不足（n=3）、缺 homo/hetero panel 对照、kill criteria 操作定义模糊、K_eff 公式本身未验证。已提交 Director。\n\n**投稿目标**: AAAI 2027 abstract 2026-07-21, full 2026-07-28（~2 个月）。\n\n**Why:** 首次审查的发现如果不修正，MVP 的 pass/fail 判断将不可信——统计功效不足可能导致虚假 pass。\n**How to apply:** 后续跟踪 Director/Agent 是否采纳了 homo/hetero 对照组、扩展 K 值、明确 MI 估计方法。\n",

    "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 → 4.6, 4.6 → 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a pull request\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",
